What the author
wrote, may his Light
shine <note>original: "neru," a traditional Hebrew blessing for a living person</note>

In this chapter, we shall explain, with the help of the Blessed Name, the customs of the communities. Several holy communities have practiced <gloss>in this</gloss>, and this custom has spread regarding the matter of blessings and prayers. The entirety of their words is based upon the words of the legal decisors <note>original: "poskim," rabbis whose legal rulings are authoritative</note> of blessed memory. <insert>And see the book "Shem HaGedolim"</insert> <note>A famous bibliographic work by Rabbi Chaim Joseph David Azulai</note>. And so it appears to me, for this is the path where light dwells, and with this, we shall come to the explanation. For one must divide this into several categories. And behold, <gloss>according to</gloss> what our rabbis have written, the law is with them, particularly in this matter <unclear>which</unclear> pertains to the subject of the Festivals. I likewise found written in an <unclear>old</unclear> book, and these are the <unclear>aforementioned</unclear> words: "Things that were said in the name of Rabbi So-and-so <unclear>of blessed memory</unclear>." This requires great study, for all his words are words of truth and justice. <unclear>And we</unclear> have practiced <unclear>it</unclear> according to our custom from days of old, and we shall not change the custom, Heaven forbid. And as for what the aforementioned Rabbi wrote, and what appears to my own weak opinion—the one teaching and speaking here—my heart tells me that it is so, and my colleagues <unclear>who heard me</unclear> agreed with me. I relied in this matter on what I wrote in <unclear>this</unclear> section, and so it appears.

# On the Matter of the Word

What was explained regarding the matter of the blessing when one <unclear>prays</unclear> in a congregation or in private; for the Blessed Name hears the prayer of every mouth. This is explicitly stated in the books of the ancient sages of blessed memory. Whatever requires further study in this and what pertains to this location, I have explained it all in my previous book. There you will find satisfaction, and anyone seeking an answer will find it in this book with the help of Heaven <note>original: "bs"d," an abbreviation for 'BeSiyata DeShmaya'</note>, for it is the key, and this is sufficient.

<meta>The catchword "And with this" from the previous page connects to the opening discussion on the importance of action. This page transitions into a commentary on the Torah portion of Shelach.</meta>

...And this is because "everything is according to the multitude of action" <note>A quote from Pirkei Avot 3:15, suggesting that character is shaped by repetitive good deeds</note>, and specifically so, for our Sages of Blessed Memory <note>original: "Chazal"</note> said that their words are <unclear>living</unclear>. Even though they were expressed with brevity of speech, behold they are very deep and broad. Nachmanides <note>Rabbi Moses ben Nachman, 13th-century scholar; original: "Ramban"</note>, may his righteous memory be for a blessing, wrote regarding that verse that the essence of the "action" is the act of obedience to the Blessed One. May it be the will of the Blessed Name that He merit us to walk in His straight paths, to serve Him with a whole heart and a willing soul, Amen Selah, and may it be His will.

# Shelach
<note>The Torah portion "Shelach Lecha" (Numbers 13:1–15:41), which tells the story of the twelve spies sent to scout the Land of Canaan.</note>

"Send for yourself men, that they may scout the land of Canaan, which I give..." (Numbers 13:2). Behold, one must examine this closely, for it is known that the commandments of the Torah are truth and justice for every living being. Yet Rashi <note>The primary medieval French commentator</note>, of blessed memory, wrote as follows: "'Send for yourself'—according to your own judgment; I do not command you; if you wish, send." Is it conceivable that the Blessed Name would say to him regarding a matter from which evil would result, "If you wish, send"? For the Blessed Name desires only good in His world!

Rather, the simple excuse <note>or "resolution"</note> is that in truth, their souls were very great, even above the level of Moses our Teacher, peace be upon him. This is the intent of Rashi's specific wording, "according to your judgment"—meaning that all their knowledge, wisdom, and attributes were above your own judgment and intellect. Therefore, the Holy One, Blessed be He, said, "Send for yourself according to your judgment," for if *He* had sent them, an immeasurably great evil would have resulted. However, Moses our Teacher, peace be upon him, saw in his prophecy that among these men were wicked individuals, etc. For this reason, he did not want to send them until he consulted with the Divine Presence <note>original: "Shekhinah"</note>, and the Holy One, Blessed be He, said to him, "Send for yourself according to your judgment."

Rashi, of blessed memory, explained: "According to your judgment," etc. We must understand what Rashi's intention was here. Why would it matter to the Holy One, Blessed be He, whether he sends them or not? Just because the people requested it, was Moses forced to send them? Could the Holy One, Blessed be He, not have prevented this thing from them? Rather, certainly the intention is as written in the holy book <term>Or HaChayim</term> <note>A famous 18th-century Torah commentary by Rabbi Chaim ibn Attar</note>: "And Moses sent them..." It is proper to understand how we find, etc. The matter is that the souls of these spies were very, very high in their level; only later did they become corrupted through pride, etc. This is why the Holy One, Blessed be He, said to him, "Send for yourself," for in truth there was a great good in this. And even though there was great suffering for Israel, nevertheless, it was all for the good, as it is written. And with this, it will be understood...

<summary>This page discusses the spiritual nature of the spies sent to Canaan, arguing that they originally possessed exceptionally high souls but were later corrupted by pride. It reconciles God's permission to send the spies with the tragic outcome by suggesting the act was ultimately for a greater good.</summary>
<keywords>Shelach, Spies, Moses, Rashi, Or HaChayim, Nachmanides, free will, pride, soul levels, Land of Israel</keywords>

### 49

What are the "complete" things, etc.? This is like what Rav Pappa said: **"Increase your teachers and merit it"** <note>original Aramaic: "Kohl rabach v'zachi beh." A Talmudic maxim suggesting that by consulting multiple authorities or being more rigorous, one ensures the law is decided correctly.</note>, for the essence is not found in the **Torah** <note>Meaning it is a Rabbinic law rather than a direct Biblical decree</note>, for they said the "complete" is eighty-four thousand <note>The significance of this number likely refers to the vast amount of oral traditions or specific legal details</note> and more and more, and not even one should be missing. And thus ruled the **Rama** <term>Rama<gloss>Rabbi Moses Isserles (1520–1572), the primary authority for Ashkenazi Jewish law</gloss></term> in his book **The Laws of the Sin Offering** <term>Torat Hattat<gloss>A major work on dietary laws by the Rama</gloss></term>.

And in section 64, paragraph 7, he wrote: if they placed **forbidden fat** <term>Chelev<gloss>Specific animal fats that the Torah prohibits for consumption, as opposed to "shuman," which is permitted fat</gloss></term> inside the stomach when there is **milk** <note>In Hebrew, the words for "forbidden fat" and "milk" are spelled identically (חלב). The context determines if it is "Chelev" (fat) or "Chalav" (milk)</note> in it, or if we say that even though the animal was found to be a **torn animal** <term>Treifah<gloss>An animal with a physical defect that makes it unkosher even if slaughtered correctly</gloss></term>, the law is simple: at the outset, if it is from a cow, it is permitted. And as for an animal born via Caesarean section ~~if~~ also the opinion of the **Rama** in his gloss is that the intestines of an animal—such as the rectum, the omasum, the entrails, the stomach, and the small intestines—the fat upon them is permitted <gloss>pure fat</gloss>.

And if we say here that one cooked it, the law is as the **Rama** wrote in section 81, paragraph 6, as follows: "The stomach of a **carcass** <term>Nevelah<gloss>An animal that died without proper ritual slaughter</gloss></term> or a **torn animal** <term>Treifah<gloss>An animal with a terminal illness or injury</gloss></term> is considered as if it were gathered in a vessel." If [the milk] was separated inside it, it is kosher. The reason is that the milk within it is not distinct and is not mixed with the meat of the stomach. And in section 87, paragraph 4, he wrote: a stomach cooked in milk is permitted, even if it belongs to a carcass or a torn animal. And if one salted it ~~with the~~ even though there is milk in it, it is kosher.

The law here at the end of section 102 is in this language: and the glosses of the **Rama**, of blessed memory, wrote that one does not make cheese in the stomach of an animal slaughtered not according to the law, or in the stomach of a torn animal. But if one did so, it is kosher after the fact. And if one burned the milk in the stomach, it is like milk during slaughter ~~that is forbidden~~.

And behold, here in this incident, it is very difficult, for how could you ~~not~~ have written the manner of the incident, how ~~it was done and what its name was~~ the incident occurred? If they placed the milk of the stomach into the pot with the water and the cow's milk, the law is simple: if it is a dry substance, it is nullified in a ratio of **sixty to one** <note>The "Bitul b'shishim" rule, where a prohibited substance is nullified if the permitted substance is sixty times its volume</note>, and if it is liquid, it requires a **thin peel's** <note>Kelippah—a legal requirement to remove a thin layer of the food</note> worth of removal. It makes no difference if it is perforated or not perforated. And if the milk within it was foul, the **Legal Decisors** <note>Poskim</note> wrote that even if it was cooked with the milk, it is kosher and permitted to eat.

And regarding this, he said the law is simple <margin>section 81</margin> in the name of the **Ra'avad** <note>Rabbi Abraham ben David, a 12th-century commentator</note>. And this is the ruling that Rav Pappa said: "Increase your teacher and merit it," as the **Rama** wrote in section 81, paragraph 6, that the stomach, etc. And it was already written above that meat is perfectly fine, and the stomach of a carcass, etc. And thus ruled our teacher and master, **Rabbi Yehuda Halawa** in his book **Justice and Charity** <term>Mishpat U-Tzedaka<gloss>A work of Rabbinic responsa or legal rulings</gloss></term>, letter P. And peace.

### 50

Mi

The **instructions** <term>Hora'ot<gloss>Legal rulings or spiritual directions provided by Rabbinic authorities</gloss></term> of the **teachers** <term>Morim<gloss>Religious instructors or legal decisors</gloss></term> must stand first and foremost. Otherwise, no **excuse** <term>Teretz<gloss>A justification or legal defense for one's actions</gloss></term> would help a person, even if he had one thirty-three times over <note>original: "LG mal" (לג מאל). The Hebrew letters Lamed-Gimmel represent the number 33, likely used here as a figurative expression for 'many times' or perhaps referring to a specific set of justifications.</note>. And indeed, this is the matter that he says here in brief: that he would have [attained his goal] if one only needs to hear it from the one who is the smallest among them all. For he writes here regarding the **commandment** <term>Mitzvah<gloss>A religious duty or divine decree</gloss></term> itself: "And every **scholar** <term>Chacham<gloss>Literally a 'wise man,' referring to a learned person or a Torah sage</gloss></term> who engages in the Torah..." but is the scholar exempt from all other commandments? Rather, the scholar specifically must do this, because he must make his **soul** <term>Neshamah<gloss>The divine spark or spiritual essence within a human being</gloss></term> more blissful. 

This is the main thing a person must do here in this world, and after this, he need not fear any other person in this world, because he will have to give an account to his **Creator** <term>Borei<gloss>A common title for God emphasizing the act of creation</gloss></term>. And this is the main point that should stand in the **verse** <term>Pasuk<gloss>A sentence or passage from the Hebrew Bible</gloss></term>: "Sanctify." His **holiness** <term>Kedushah<gloss>Spiritual elevation or sanctity</gloss></term> must be such that his whole heart belongs entirely to God, blessed be He, and he must not think of anything else. If he is a learned man, he must have a pure intention in his **prayer** <term>Tefillah<gloss>Structured worship or individual communication with the Divine</gloss></term>. And he must also allow himself to be given **moral instruction** <term>Musar<gloss>Ethical rebuke, discipline, or spiritual correction</gloss></term> even by one who is lesser than him; for if he does this, he will be very beloved by God, blessed be He, and his reward will be great there in the **World to Come** <term>Olam Ha-Ba<gloss>The spiritual realm of the afterlife or the future messianic era</gloss></term>.

<column-break/>

Shi

The rest that is written here in the verse, one must learn from it. This is a great matter that the **Sages** <note>original: "Chachamim"</note> have taught us: that we should only be **pious** <term>Frum<gloss>Devout, God-fearing, or strictly observant of Jewish law</gloss></term> and that we should be good to God and to other people. For this is the essence of the entire Torah: that we should do good and not evil. May the Eternal One help us to **fulfill** <term>Mekayem<gloss>To uphold, sustain, or put into practice a religious obligation</gloss></term> all of this. May we be seen in joy, Amen.

<summary>This page outlines the ethical duties of a Torah scholar, emphasizing that true holiness requires a pure heart in prayer and the humility to accept moral instruction from anyone, regardless of their status. It concludes that the fundamental goal of the Torah is to be good to both God and fellow human beings.</summary>
<keywords>scholarship, humility, moral instruction, World to Come, piety, soul refinement, Torah essence</keywords>

Secondly, when he told him to speak and to invite his friend to come and hear who his relative was—rather, it is written that as a matter of course, when he spoke within the community in the presence of many people according to the **laws** <term>Dinim<gloss>The established rules of Jewish jurisprudence</gloss></term>, such is the case before us. <margin>16</margin>
Behold, regarding what the examiner wrote in *Baruch Hu* <note>Likely a reference to the work "Baruch Ta'am" or a similar commentary.</note> concerning the words of **Rabbi Shimon** <term>Rashba<gloss>Rabbi Solomon ben Abraham ibn Adret, a major 13th-century authority</gloss></term>, of blessed memory, who said that everything depends on the **discretion of the judge** <term>Re'ot Einei Ha-Dayan<gloss>Literally "the sight of the judge's eyes"; judicial discretion based on the specific circumstances of a case</gloss></term>, the explanation of his words is simple, as mentioned above. Since the judge has a **proven assessment** <term>Umdana de-Mokhach<gloss>A strong legal presumption based on circumstantial evidence</gloss></term> that the matters were done by way of jest and mockery—as the **Ran** <term>Ran<gloss>Rabbi Nissim of Gerona, a 14th-century commentator</gloss></term>, of blessed memory, wrote there in the aforementioned Chapter 10 of the *Author*—the phrase "everything is according to the judge's view" indicates the difference between one case and another, and between one person and another. And since the jesting was written as being in a way of joke and laughter, the **betrothal** <term>Kiddushin<gloss>The first stage of the Jewish marriage process, which requires a formal divorce to dissolve</gloss></term> is rightly nullified according to everyone. And so wrote the **Mordechai** <term>Mordechai<gloss>A 13th-century legal compendium by Mordechai ben Hillel</gloss></term> in the first chapter in the name of **Rashbam** <term>Rashbam<gloss>Rabbi Samuel ben Meir, a grandson of Rashi</gloss></term>: that likewise, if everyone sees that it was done in a way of jest, such as if it occurred at a wedding feast or other days of joy, these are not betrothals at all. They certainly showed a source for this. And what **Maimonides** <term>Rambam<gloss>Rabbi Moses ben Maimon</gloss></term> and the **SeMag** <term>SeMag<gloss>Sefer Mitzvot Gadol, a 13th-century legal work</gloss></term> wrote in Chapter 3 of the *Laws of Betrothal* follows the general words of the Sages.

<meta>Continuity: While the previous page concluded a legal discussion regarding a marriage dispute in Regensburg and public rumors, this page transitions into a philosophical and theological discourse. It shifts from legal rulings to an exploration of Divine providence, citing Maimonides' "Guide for the Perplexed" and the Zohar to explain how God moves the universe.</meta>

The intention in this verse—and we shall speak of what the Guide <note>original: "Ha-Moreh" (המורה), referring to Maimonides (Rambam) and his philosophical work *The Guide for the Perplexed*.</note> explained—is that all existing beings are entirely in the hand of God, like a covenant that was made, etc. And it goes round and round, once above and a second time below, according to every matter and circumstance, and according to the place and time in which it exists. For this reason, he mentioned the movers—the totality of the moving forces and the totality of the objects being moved—for He, may He be blessed, is the **Absolute Mover** <term>Absolute Mover<gloss>A philosophical concept, famously used by Maimonides, describing God as the first cause of all motion and existence in the universe</gloss></term>. Every other force acts by the hand of God; likewise, the luminaries and the entire system of the heavens. 

He "cut" and "wrote"—and what is the explanation of this? "And He wrote the heavens and all their host," while the Master <note>original: "Ha-M'" (המ'), likely an abbreviation for *Ha-Melekh* (The King) or *Ha-Meni'a* (The Mover).</note>, may He be blessed, spoke and it was. Regarding this, our Sages of blessed memory <term>Chazal<gloss>An acronym for "Chachameinu Zichronam Livracha," the Jewish sages of the Mishnah and Talmud</gloss></term> said that the verse states: "And the Lord said to Noah:
<margin>20</margin>
'Come, you and all your household' [into the Ark]," etc. Could He not have brought him in without an act of entry? Rather, it is because He is the aspect of the "Mover-Place" <note>A philosophical-mystical term suggesting God as the "space" in which the world exists.</note> who dwells within time, and the Living One <note>original: "Ha-Ch'" (והח'), likely *Ha-Chai* (The Living One).</note> who gazes. The Master, may He be blessed, is the aspect of **Judgment** <term>Din<gloss>The attribute of Divine justice, law, and structure</gloss></term>, and the Living One watches over the creatures of the world and moves [them], may He be blessed. Here the word of the verse is completed, and regarding this they brought proof from salt—salt without [end?]. The ultimate purpose of the speaking verse is that there is intention and will from the Leader <note>original: "Ha-Manhig" (המנהיג), a term for God as the governor of the universe.</note>, may He be blessed.

---

<margin>20</margin>
The simple explanation is that His entire will, may He be blessed, is primary and is beyond searching out. Therefore, a person should not ask, "Why did God do this?" for the human intellect is too short to grasp the absolute cause in every single thing. But behold, everything is by His word, and its explanation is very broad according to the understanding of the verses. This is what the verse means: "And the earth was
<margin>21</margin>
unformed and void, and darkness was upon the face of the deep," etc. All the explanations said regarding this are only what accords with human intellect, but the truth is as is known: "**Everything is in the hands of Heaven except the fear of Heaven**" <note>A famous quote from the Talmud (Berakhot 33b) meaning that while God controls the world, humans have free will in their moral and spiritual choices.</note>, for everything follows from His leadership, may He be blessed. 

If a person were to observe himself—what is his power? Would he not see that in His <note>God's</note> hand is the **abundance** <term>Shefa<gloss>The divine flow or influence that sustains and gives life to all existence</gloss></term> and the life-force that brings him to every desired thing, and from Him is everything. Just as the soul gives life to the body, so the **Divine Presence** <term>Shekhinah<gloss>The feminine, indwelling aspect of God that resides within the physical world</gloss></term> gives life to all worlds, as it is written in the Zohar <note>The foundational work of Jewish mysticism (Kabbalah).</note>: "For there is no place empty of Him." <note>original: "De-ha leit atar panui mineih" (Aramaic).</note> By this, the careful and correct examiner will understand that behold, every action that is performed is by His hand, may He be blessed. Man has nothing but the **choice** <term>Bechirah<gloss>Free will; the human capacity to choose between good and evil</gloss></term> between good and evil. God shall guard all who walk in integrity. May the one who understands grasp the abundance of these stories and why the Sages saw fit to mention these deeds in this book. Indeed, a person should never weary at any moment from speaking only in praise of the **Absolute Place** <term>Ha-Makom<gloss>Literally "The Place"; a rabbinic name for God, implying that God is the place of the world, but the world is not His place (omnipresence)</gloss></term>, blessed be He and blessed be His name.

<summary>This page explores the philosophy of Divine providence, arguing that God is the "Absolute Mover" of all things while maintaining that human beings possess free will (fear of Heaven). It draws on Maimonides' "Guide for the Perplexed" and the Zohar to explain how the Divine Presence (Shekhinah) animates the world like a soul animates a body.</summary>

<keywords>Absolute Mover, Divine Providence, Maimonides (Rambam), Shekhinah, Zohar, Free Will, Noah's Ark, Shefa, Fear of Heaven</keywords>

And they must write as it has been explained; and also my master and teacher <note>original: "Mar u-Maran" (מר ומרן), a title of high respect for a rabbinic authority</note>, may his memory live in the life of the world to come, [ruled] even regarding contracts that contain a standing signature, which were made based on the "two-out-of-three" rule <note>original: "chevel bet chazit" (חבל ב' חזית), likely a technical legal shorthand for witness verification or property measurement</note> regarding contracts. In such a case, everyone agrees that the one who says it is "valid" [is correct], as we wrote later. And the writing is common, and in the second book of writing, and it requires further investigation. And regarding this, it appears he learned from Maimonides <note>original: "Ha-Rambam" (הרמב"ם)</note>, of blessed memory, in Chapter 8 of the Laws of [Sales], who wrote that if a person uses force <term>Allem<gloss>A legal term for a "strongman" or someone using coercion/intimidation</gloss></term> against his fellow to make him sell to him, even though he did not give him the proper value of the <insert>money</insert>, we compel the seller [to fulfill the sale] because he needs it. And why would the seller agree to the sale if it was signed and sealed by witnesses of blessed memory? For it is possible that he will not return with a protest <term>Ir'ur<gloss>A legal challenge or appeal against the current status of property ownership</gloss></term>, and they will not see it, perhaps by the hand of the witnesses of blessed memory. And it is found that if they wrote for him that it was "leased" and he did not come with them in any matter, etc. They saw those thousands that Nahmanides <note>original: "Ha-Ramban" (הרמב"ן)</note> of blessed memory wrote in the chapter "The Presumptive Ownership of Property" <note>original: "Chezkath" (חזקת), referring to the third chapter of Tractate Bava Batra</note>, where he wrote that in the three years of Reuben in this matter, you certainly retracted your claim. He said there are five types of distinctions regarding that [sale] during the three years. And likewise, "it is life"—this refers to the sale, lest he return to him, and he returned on a festival during the three years of [occupation]. And in the dispute of Rabbi Solomon <note>original: "R' Shlomo," likely Rashi or Solomon ben Adret (Rashba)</note> in the name of the "Book of the Nest" <note>original: "Sefer Kan" (ספר קן)</note>, it stands as if he admitted [to the sale], and the protester did not object during the eight... etc., that he benefited from the life during the three years and did not retract. Behold, the three years of Reuben over those of [Simeon] have passed. And we see for ourselves, what did you see? That he is obligated to reflect upon it. And it is written in the book *Even Ha-Ezer* <note>The "Stone of Help," a major halakhic work</note> that she insisted, and he divorces her in such a case. And I am afraid of these laws of monetary matters <term>Dinei Mamonot<gloss>Jewish civil laws dealing with money, property, and contracts</gloss></term>, and there is truth in the three [years]. It was established according to the rule and the principle, he did not leave [it], and afterward in the fifth year, because in the years that are years <insert>it is void</insert>.

And so ruled our teacher Rabbi Pinchas Sar-Shalom Ha-Kohen <note>A 14th-century Ashkenazi authority</note> of blessed memory in his Responsa, Sign 8, and these are his words: "Since the owners have become desperate, he asked him if it came as a deposit <term>Pikadon<gloss>An object left in the trust of another, not intended as a gift or sale</gloss></term> or in a response. And that which you asked regarding the two wives, his mouth was closed regarding selling or to sell to him, and they merit them, and even within the three years correctly. And may there be true peace, lest he have the power to stand up and protest," etc.—

And behold, the measure of two years and going out is certainly [part] of the three years of the cantor <note>original: "Chazan" (חזן), possibly referring to a specific communal official or a typo for "Chazakah" (presumption)</note>, and it should be written. All of them, of blessed memory, [concluded] that even two [years] that are divided within the three years, after three years... and all those written in the tractate *Chullin* <note>The Talmudic tractate dealing with dietary laws, but here likely cited for a specific legal principle</note> of blessed memory, etc., even in the chapter "One Who Sells the House" <note>The fourth chapter of Tractate Bava Batra</note>, etc. And furthermore, that the time is complete. And then he relied upon him, of blessed memory, and wrote: "Behold, in my three years," etc. From this, because of the three years, and that it is not according to the law of the custom, of blessed memory. And also the contracts of the owners are in the hand of the buyer, he retracted these two [or] three years of the owners. And one could say that there is no presumptive ownership... as explained, and in short, everything is written.

<meta>Continuity: The previous page discussed legal procedures for contracts and property challenges (Ir’ur), specifically citing Maimonides. This page continues the legal analysis by contrasting the opinions of the Talmudic sages Rav Yehuda and Rava regarding property lost at sea, before transitioning into a moral discourse on divine providence and the character of the righteous.</meta>

<margin>23.</margin>
And this is what we <unclear>explained</unclear> above regarding what has come before us now. For in truth, by the letter of the law, all of this should have belonged to him. However, perhaps it can be said that since it has already <unclear>sunk</unclear> into the sea, it no longer belonged to him at all. Therefore, Rav Yehuda <note>A prominent Babylonian sage of the 3rd century, known for his expertise in civil law</note> argued correctly, even though we previously said in the Gemara <note>The component of the Talmud consisting of rabbinic analysis and commentary on the Mishnah</note> that this is not the case. Rather, here it was stated correctly: in any place where this occurs, it is because it had no substance from the beginning. But here, since it <unclear>originally</unclear> had substance, [Rav] Yehuda was of the opinion that it is treated like the loss of <unclear>property</unclear>. However, Rava <note>One of the most famous Babylonian rabbis, known for his legal debates with Abaye</note> holds that even in this case, it is not so. And this is why he concluded that the same law applies to all the aforementioned cases, for this is the well-known principle in this matter. Anyone who does not act accordingly is included among those who "do an evil deed." For if he does so intentionally, he is liable according to the Torah; and even if it is unintentional, he still requires atonement. This is what they said in the Midrash of our Sages, of blessed memory <note>original: "Midrash Chazal" (מדרש חז"ל)</note>, on the verse: "And He, being merciful, forgives iniquity" <note>Psalm 78:38</note>. With this, the matter is completed for the time being. May the pleasantness [of God be upon us].

---

<margin>24.</margin>
Now let us return to the first matter we addressed. It is worthy to be known and publicized to every person—and even if it is not publicly known as it should be, nevertheless, the truth testifies for itself: whoever possesses a good heart and a "good eye" <note>A Hebrew idiom for generosity and a positive outlook on others</note> will merit all the good virtues. Even if an obstacle occasionally befalls him, the Holy One, Blessed be He, rescues him from it. For "one who comes to purify himself is assisted from Heaven" <note>A famous Talmudic maxim (Shabbat 104a) suggesting that divine aid follows good intentions</note>. We find this with Rabbi Phinehas ben Jair <note>A 2nd-century sage renowned for his extreme piety and the legends of his miraculous donkey</note>, who was very careful in all his words and deeds. Even with his donkey, miracles and wonders were seen. For one who is attached to the Blessed Name is above the laws of nature; whatever he says, God fulfills and it rises as a favorable offering before the King of kings, the Holy One, Blessed be He. This is known to all who possess "grace and true knowledge" <note>original: "Yodei Chen," a term often used to refer to students of Kabbalah or hidden wisdom</note>.

---

<margin>25.</margin>
There is further need to awaken [the heart] regarding this, as we often see the "righteous man perishing in his righteousness, and the wicked man prolonging his life in his evil" <note>A reference to Ecclesiastes 7:15, addressing the problem of why bad things happen to good people</note>. In this matter, there are great secrets that we have no permission to question. For "the ways of the Lord are straight" <note>Hosea 14:10</note> and all His works are done in faithfulness. If we do not grasp His intellect, it is because of our limited understanding. For He, may He be blessed, sees from afar what we cannot see. Sometimes, what appears to a person as bad is actually for his benefit, Heaven forbid [to think otherwise]. For "whatever the Merciful One does, He does for the good" <note>original: "Kol mai d'avid Rachmana l'tav avid" (כל מאי דעביד רחמנא לטב עביד), a famous Aramaic aphorism from the Talmud</note>. This principle must be engraved on the heart of every member of Israel, for it is the foundation of complete faith. If one merits this, he is a "son of the World to Come." Through this, he can endure everything that passes over him with joy and love, as it is said: "Serve the Lord with joy" <note>Psalm 100:2</note>. May the Holy One, Blessed be He, help us for the sake of the glory of His Name.

<meta>Continuity: The previous page concluded a legal discussion regarding property rights and moral liability. Previously it was said that "depart," "turned away," and "their stench" <note>original: "sur, sar, sericho" (סור, סר, סריכו) — etymological variations of turning away or corruption</note> and regarding them in <unclear>clean</unclear> language and because I came...
regarding what belongs to Him, may He be blessed, within it, and that this is He, may He be blessed, and that this is an attribute used in human language, and that...
The name is mentioned there because you cannot think of either Eternity <note>original: "Netzach" (נצח) — the seventh of the ten Sefirot, or Divine emanations, associated with endurance</note> or Splendor <note>original: "Hod" (הוד) — the eighth Sefirah, associated with majesty and submission</note> as lacking power in those two gates.

->---<-
->around the <unclear>Intellect</unclear><-

And now we return to the matter... for four things <unclear>were said</unclear> regarding Him, may He be blessed, for He is considered in His essence and His unity. 
The Sage <note>Likely referring to Maimonides (the Rambam), who famously articulated this triad in his work 'Mishneh Torah'</note>, may He be blessed and exalted, said of Him that He is **the Intellect**, and He is **the Knower**, and He is **the Known**.
To the truth of His wisdom in Himself, etc. Behold, His wisdom is in His Kingship <note>original: "Malkhuto" (מלכותו) — referring to the tenth Sefirah, through which the Divine interacts with the physical world</note> and there is nothing outside of Him at all in His truth.
And behold, He possesses Wisdom, Understanding, and Knowledge <note>original: "Chokhmah, Binah, Da'at" (חכמה, בינה, דעת) — the three 'intellectual' Sefirot</note>, and also an account of His attributes in <unclear>every</unclear> word.
In this it is hinted that He is in Understanding and Strength, and Understanding and Greatness <note>original: "Binah, Gevurah, Gedulah" — Kabbalistic attributes</note>, for He is the Intellect and the Soul of the <unclear>world</unclear>.
As we have heard, He is the vitality of everything and He gives life to everything, and is the Soul of all hidden souls.
And he stated a foundation in this matter that he explained, and from his words it appears that the meaning of these <unclear>words</unclear> of his are well-known, and that he is exalted in a great degree. This necessitates, according to this, that Wisdom, Understanding, and Knowledge and all the Gates in the Upper Realm, etc., are four things as we have stated. And also according to this, it appears that action and speech are also part of this matter. And we say "prophet" and "shall come" and "they shall come" <note>Hebrew: "navi, yavo, yavo’u"</note>—the eight and eight [letters/words] found in the Torah portion *V'Zot HaBerakhah* <note>The final portion of the Book of Deuteronomy</note>.
As he wrote, there are eight names of three letters of the [Divine] Name, and four of the second Name, and corresponding to them eight others in the names of the man. In this general rule, it appears that two names are hinted at in this way, <unclear>and he wrote</unclear> in it A, B, Y <note>The letters Aleph, Bet, Yod</note>.
The part of the second name is Understanding (*Binah*); the letter *Vav* is the High Exalted Crown <note>original: "Keter" (כתר) — the highest Sefirah, representing the Divine Will</note> and the <unclear>Understanding</unclear> of holiness; and Understanding is the number eight <note>The Hebrew letter 'Chet' (ח) has the numerical value of 8</note> corresponding to the Upper One upon its structure below it. And Knowledge (*Da'at*) is the decider, and it is the Foundation <note>original: "Yesod" (יסוד) — the ninth Sefirah</note> of Understanding and the holy <unclear>Soul</unclear>, and the stronghold of stones of light. From it comes forth [the letters] A, D, M <note>Spells 'Adam' (אדם), or Man</note> and it is for the simple unity; there is no division in them, Heaven forbid, but all is one and there is no separation at all, Heaven forbid and far be it.
He wrote in the name of the Rabbi, of blessed memory, and the pious one, regarding his wisdom and understanding, and wrote that the matter of the Name <unclear>is</unclear> not hinted at regarding God—

->---<-

The explanation of the name *YaH* <note>A shortened form of the Tetragrammaton, the four-letter name of God</note> which is in Understanding, for *YaH* is the Upper Crown... and he is the Name. And he explained and understood the four *Sefirot* <note>The emanations of Divine light</note> as in the matter of the honored and awesome Name, may He be blessed and exalted forever, Amen.
And the secret of the verse <unclear>in Understanding</unclear> is that the four directions of the world came out from Him with the power of the Exalted Holy One: Kingship, Crown, and Understanding. And regarding them it is said: "Blessed be the glory of the Lord from His place" <note>Ezekiel 3:12</note>. This "Place" refers to the four-letter Name [Y-H-W-H].
For this is the matter of Wisdom, Understanding, and Knowledge. And he explained that the matter of this letter *Aleph* (א) is a hint to His unity, may He be blessed, who teaches His wisdom in His love. In it, he explained that the matter of the Crown (*Keter*) is the First Hidden Light.
And since Wisdom, Understanding, and Knowledge are one, behold Crown is the Head, and it is the Light with which the world was created.

<margin>As it appears to me <note>original abbreviation: "K.L."</note></margin>
Behold, from all the words of the aforementioned early authorities <note>original: "Rishonim"</note>, may their memory be a blessing, it appears that since according to Torah law, written testimony is equivalent to oral testimony—and even though it is written "from their mouths" and not "from their writing," this applies specifically to the court in the first instance <note>original: "lekhatchila"</note>. However, in a situation of duress, or when one recognizes the handwriting, it is completely valid according to the opinion of the Ramban, may his memory be a blessing. And such appears to be the opinion of the Tosafot <note>Medieval commentators on the Talmud</note>, may their memory be a blessing, in Tractate Gittin, page 71. And this is especially true in matters of a woman's testimony, where the law was made more lenient for her sake to prevent her from being "chained" <note>original: "Aguna"; a woman unable to remarry because her husband's death or location is unverified</note>. Furthermore, the main principle is that when [the witness] is unable to come to court and his testimony is necessary, this is similar to what is written in Tractate Bava Kamma, page 112b, that the courts send their scribes, and everything similar to this. And the words of the Rama in Section 17, Paragraph 13, are true and established.

<margin>To [Section] 31 <note>original: "La-aleph"</note></margin>
I have further found that in the words of the aforementioned early authorities, may their memory be a blessing, it was explained that even though we hold the principle that "one does not act to a person's disadvantage except in their presence," nevertheless, in a matter that provides a person a benefit—such as exempting them from the obligation of an oath and the like—this is considered a complete merit, and it is possible to testify not in their presence, as stated in the chapter "The One who Receives" <margin>and as written</margin> regarding the law of the "third party," may his memory be a blessing. These are ancient matters <note>i.e., well-established laws</note>, and it is sufficient to be brief here regarding a matter that is already explained.

<meta>Continuity: This page represents a sharp departure from the previous page's technical legal index regarding the admissibility of hearsay in divorce cases. It returns to the homiletic and ethical themes seen earlier in the manuscript, specifically investigating the spiritual nature of "good news" and the emotional sincerity required of a messenger.</meta>

Blessed is He who blesses commoners <note>original: "hedyotot"; refers to ordinary people without high social or scholarly standing</note>, for good news is pleasant to hear, and even to an ordinary person, it appears like a true message, and so on. 
And when they informed him of her welfare, and so on, particularly regarding matters of the commandments, and particularly for a message from God, and particularly
for this person or that person; and it is even more excellent since it is sufficient, and even to bring news to the "face of Moses which is like the face of the sun" <note>A traditional idiom referring to a person of great spiritual radiance or leadership</note>, and such things as this.
Because it is written that the "heart is in joy," and since this appears good to them, it is possible that it is not true joy. 
And even if the heart of the one who blesses is [sincere], what if these <unclear>inner hearts</unclear> and the joy in their actions are with complete joy, and so on? And afterward,
there are many people who are happy from the heart—the "inner heart"—and some say it is a commandment for him to gladden his heart with complete joy. 
And if he does not care about the person, it is not truly in his soul, for God forbid the law of the Torah would be to punish him. And regarding 
this, God forbid, the Rabbinical Court <note>original: "B.D." / "Beit Din"</note> has said, as it were, while the wicked man rejoices in his deeds, that they could use this to execute judgment upon them.
And for this reason, he spoke "words of delight" only to the messenger. And therefore, "comfort of kindness" was expressed, which brought out grace and a good word, 
so that they would understand that the intention of messengers and their words are not good unless the speaker is good with a good heart. 
And because of this, when one comes to bring news, one should bring news to the "face of a man," for with him is the confusion; and he removed the heart and raised 
his face in joy, like the "face of Moses." And he wrote what the <term>Sages of Faith</term> <note>original: "Hachmei Emunah"; likely a reference to Kabbalists or ethical mystics</note> said: once he heard that all was well, and so on, God forbid, and this is 
about the "heart in joy." And there are those who wrote what they wrote for the sake of the commoners as "good words," and one conducts oneself with them 
in this language with joy, and so on. And because of this, we need a messenger to bring him news and be seen in his house. 
And for this, the law is that a "joyful heart" is the best thing to bring desire and the joy of a pious person, and so on. —

<margin>Heart</margin>
The heart—and here he comes to inform us that the heart is not complete in this tidings unless he sees the face of the messenger, and he 
is of a bitter countenance. Therefore, if he comes to inform us of joyful things from the heart of the messenger, his heart will be excellent to do good for this.
Therefore, it is considered a joy to write of the joy specifically for the heart of the messenger, that he should rejoice in his deeds because he desires 
kindness and the "Life of Lives" <note>A poetic reference to God</note> and joy and peace, etc. And we have said in the name of Rabbi Judah that the one who brings good news does so with the joy 
of the heart. Therefore, when the messenger of peace comes to the heart of the messenger, such is the law: that it is not complete, God forbid, 
for the sake of a commoner, etc. He explained that he was happy in his heart for the coming of the common messenger, and it is not the law to recite a blessing 
over this. But the messenger with the joy of the heart, the one who blesses his coming to announce peace, as it is written: "In peace and truth," 
etc. And regarding this tidings that we have come upon, we need to know the messenger when he comes.

<meta>The previous page's translation provided in the prompt appears to be from a different manuscript (a Christian Syriac text discussing baptism and the afterlife). However, the current Hebrew transcription is a Jewish Kabbalistic commentary focused on the mystical significance of the letter Mem, the Divine Name *Shaddai*, and the Sefirot (Divine emanations) of *Binah* (Understanding) and *Malchut* (Sovereignty). I will proceed with the translation of the Hebrew text provided while noting the thematic discontinuity with the previous summary.</meta>

Even though at the very end of existence it rests upon the letter *Mem* <note>The Hebrew letter *Mem* (מ), especially in its final form (ם), often symbolizes a closed or hidden womb in Kabbalah, representing the transition from potential to reality.</note> here, yet above in Understanding [*Binah*] <unclear>further on</unclear> in its light. This is the reason for the verse: "And by My name the LORD [Hashem] I was not known to them, but by the name God Almighty [*El Shaddai*] I appeared to them" <note>Exodus 6:3. The author is explaining why the Patriarchs experienced God through the name *Shaddai* rather than the four-letter name, the Tetragrammaton.</note> etc. One should understand from this the "First" and the "Last," and also that through the *Mem* of the Sabbath [*Shabbat*] the Torah comes.

---

The LORD; and one should consider in this the explanation of the *Mem* of Lordship [*Adonut*], upon which rests the secret of *Shaddai*. The meaning is that it becomes the "Third" in Understanding [*Binah*], and this is [the meaning of] "And My name..."
<margin>I heard this from the mouth of the Rabbi, of blessed memory.</margin>
(One could say that *El Shaddai* contains three letters *Shin* in Understanding [*Binah*]). And consider in this the Book of the Torah like the letter *He* and Understanding [*Binah*] <unclear>further on</unclear> for *El Shaddai*. And behold, we find in the Jerusalem [Talmud] in Chapter 6 that the "hundred" <note>The Hebrew letter *Kof* equals 100. This refers to complex numerical calculations (Gematria) associated with Divine Names.</note> of "I Will Be What I Will Be" [*Ehyeh Asher Ehyeh*] is in Understanding [*Binah*], and the "hundred" of "I Will Be" [*Ehyeh*] is in Understanding [*Binah*] (in the secret of the "Third" in *Binah*) etc. 
And this *Mem* comes from the pronunciation of *Shaddai*, of which there are three *Shaddai*s, and the name "the LORD, God Most High" [*El Elyon*] points to them. Through this, one can understand the secret of "And by My name the LORD I was not known to them," as it exists in Understanding [*Binah*], which is not revealed, except through *Shaddai*.
<margin>And it can be said that when the Torah was given, it was upon the secret of Understanding [*Binah*], which is the letter *He*.</margin>
In the Jerusalem [Talmud] in Chapter 6: "I heard it mentioned three times here" etc., and the scripture supports this.
<margin>And the LORD [the letter *He*] alludes to this: two *Shaddai*s in Understanding [*Binah*] and likewise *Shaddai* in Sovereignty [*Malchut*].</margin>
All is within this *Mem*; ultimately, it is the secret of the pronunciation of the name *Shaddai*. In the Jerusalem [Talmud], it is the secret of *Shaddai* in Sovereignty [*Malchut*], and there is another *Mem* of *Shaddai* in Understanding [*Binah*], and that *Mem* is not heard except in the secret of the "point" <note>The *Nikudah* (vowel point or dot), representing the most concentrated, non-verbal essence of a sound.</note>.

---

For in truth, from the perspective of the higher realm, there is the "World to Come" [*Olam Ha-Ba*], and it is the resting *Mem*, the *Mem* of the "Upper Waters" <note>original: "Mayim Elyonim". In Kabbalah, waters symbolize divine flow; the "Upper Waters" are associated with *Binah*.</note>, the secret
<margin>For Understanding [*Binah*] is called the World to Come.</margin>
of Understanding [*Binah*]. For there are two of these *Mem*s: the first corresponds to Sovereignty [*Malchut*] and the second to Understanding [*Binah*]. The secret and the pronunciation were mostly in the *Mem*, the secret of "and by My name the LORD I was not known to them," but only by the name *Shaddai* to those who seek them, etc.
And Understanding [*Binah*] is further on, the letter *He* which is Understanding [*Binah*], the secret of this *Mem*. Understand this well: even though the pronunciation is the same for both of them, nevertheless there is a distinction between them in terms of the Higher and the Final, and the Cause and the Effect.
<margin>Consider three *Shaddai*s in Sovereignty [*Malchut*] and three *Shaddai*s in Understanding [*Binah*], the secret of the *Mem* of *Shaddai*.</margin>
And behold, this *Mem* is the secret of *Shin*, *Dalet*, *Yod* [*Sh-D-Y*], with the *Mem* at their head. And behold, regarding them it is said: "and by My name the LORD I was not known to them," for they are in the secret of Understanding [*Binah*], which is not revealed, etc.

---

<margin>Explanation of the matter of the *Mem* of *Shaddai*</margin>
Know that existence rests twice upon the *Mem* because of the inner and outer appearance. 
But in Understanding [*Binah*], it rests three times. This is the secret of "and by My name the LORD I was not known to them," but only... etc.
Count this *Mem*, *Mem*, *Mem*—which are twice upon the *Mem* [of Sovereignty] and three times upon Understanding [*Binah*]. And all is a "Unique One." 
Both the *Mem* and the numerical calculation all go to one place, pointing to the secret of Unity. Also, this *Mem* points to the secret of Understanding [*Binah*], which is called "The Mother of Children" <note>original: "Im Ha-Banim". A traditional Kabbalistic title for the Sefirah of *Binah*.</note>. There she is hidden and concealed, and is not 
heard except in the secret of the Voice that emerges from the heart. She is the secret of the letter *He* of the name of the LORD [the Tetragrammaton]. Understand 
this very great and deep secret; and who can find it, if not through the secret of the True Kabbalah?

<meta>Continuity Note: This page transitions from the previous discussion on the mystical significance of the letter Lamed and Divine Names to a philosophical and Kabbalistic exploration of the relationship between the human intellect (Sechel), the soul (Nefesh/Neshamah), and the differences between humans, animals, and supernatural beings.</meta>

And as we said in the name of Rabbi Simeon <note>Rabbi Simeon bar Yochai, the traditional father of Jewish mysticism and central figure of the Zohar</note>, he says: "Behold, this is a difficult matter, for the [Divine] ability and knowledge are not connected to the head." And this nature, where life and its essence exist apart from the <term>intellect</term> <note>original: שכל — *Sechel*, referring to the rational or higher cognitive faculty</note> to such an extent that it is not [derived] from the soul, for they are a different matter. And when it is said that the intellect is found in man, and the power of the <term>Will</term> of the Creator, blessed be He, wherein are the structures of the soul for Him and for these, it lived.
And as for what was written—why is the soul of a creeping thing or a beast not [of the same] existence, and likewise why do they lack intellect, having only the power of <term>vitality</term> <note>original: חיות — *Chayyut*, the basic life force found in all living things</note>? They are weak, for from the intellect, power is strengthened. And so we see in the animal and the living creature that which is intended. And he said: We have already spoken about them, that the soul of [the beast] is firm
in the rest of the powers that lack intellect, and this is the explanation: they are nearly equal in action. To explain [man] through the intellect,
through the intellect and the soul <note>original: נשמה — *Neshamah*, the higher, divine soul distinct from the vital soul</note> that moves <note>likely meaning the soul that guides the body's actions</note> is equal to them. But this is disputed, for "intellect" was not written concerning [the animals],
and it [the text] hints at the matter of intention and expression which cannot be depicted [in animals], such as in the birds and beasts of which he spoke.
And [man] chooses the good, and like kings in Knowledge and in the intellect of the heart, they do what they desire
according to Kindness ~~from the power of the intellect~~ for the clarification of the law regarding the matter of the intellect, and the one who understands, and the one who serves—
such as humans, angels, and <term>demons</term> <note>original: שדים — *Shedim*, supernatural beings in Jewish lore often possessing both human traits, like an appetite and mortality, and angelic traits, like wings</note>, who have some [measure of] the <term>Evil Inclination</term> <note>original: יצר הרע — *Yetzer HaRa*, the innate impulse toward self-interest or sin</note>, and it is in their power to serve Him
through His commandments, and the power of the intellect to recognize the evil within them, and likewise to distance oneself from evil, especially
regarding the Torah and the commandments for which there is a reward. ~~And we further said~~ For apart from [this], it is the greatest of levels.
And if they were proven, it would be impossible for them to turn toward knowing by error of the matter to do evil, and it would be
from the power of the moving soul within their body, and in their body is the temperament of man and the tongue expressed and with power and knowledge.
~~Because it would be among the creatures and behold the Holy One, Blessed be He, [intended] for them to do so, the servant of the intellect.~~
~~We also said that for this reason he said that they have a reward in the World to Come; he spoke of the beast.~~
By their intellect, the deed is according to the intellect, because it was impossible for there to be any other speech or expression
through intellect and understanding, except by the intellect alone. And if the soul were outside of the body, it would
know everything. And the intellect which understands all words of wisdom as it originates [in] <gloss>the heart</gloss>, and the one who understands and the object of understanding,
understanding all the words of the intellect and the orders which God formed in the heart of the soul to understand through it
the precious level, and the intellect is within it. And the power of the heart is to understand and to know that he spoke [of] man,
and the soul guides the body just as one who guides a ship on the <term>Salt Sea</term> <note>original: ים המלח — *Yam HaMelach*, usually the Dead Sea, but used here as a metaphor for the turbulent material world</note>.
And he would know by the nature of the intellect, and the soul lives to do every thing that is best for the soul.

<meta>Continuity Note: This page shifts from the previous abstract, mystical discussion on the nature of the human soul and intellect to practical instructions for daily life. And these are the things one must be careful about, because they are difficult for <term>memory</term> <note>literally "cause forgetfulness"</note> and cause one to turn away from the practice as found in the <term>Midrashim</term> <note>ancient rabbinic commentaries on scripture</note> of our Sages of blessed memory: <note>original: חז"ל — *Chazal*, an acronym for "Our Sages of Blessed Memory"</note>
Studying at night in a synagogue where he never went during the day; and to sit at the entrance of the synagogue; and to pass from one synagogue to <unclear>another</unclear> synagogue.

---

A person should not accustom himself to idle chatter, but only to words of Torah, for nothing is more beautiful than it. And a person should not engage in much conversation with a woman; even with his own wife they [the Sages] said this—how much more so with the wife of his fellow. The intention is not regarding household needs, but rather idle words that lead to sin. And likewise regarding historical accounts <note>original: דברי הימים — literally "words of the days," referring to news or secular history</note> that provide no benefit to the soul but are only for the needs of the world—which one also needs to hear a bit of for worldly matters, yet everything should be in measure and weight. And also the deeds mentioned above in the previous section belong to this chapter as well, for they lead to <term>Reverence</term> <note>original: יראה — *Yirah*, often translated as "Fear of God," but implying a deep sense of awe</note>, and then he will not forget the aforementioned things. And it is written in the name of our teacher, the Ari <note>Rabbi Isaac Luria (1534–1572), the father of modern Kabbalah</note>, of righteous memory, that there are matters which are a hint to what our Sages of blessed memory said: "A man coming from a journey should not eat immediately, but rather wait a bit until his body rests." And the measure of the rest is that a man should rest from the heat of the road, and then he should drink and eat, for then the limbs will receive the benefit of the food and drink and rest, as Maimonides <note>original: הרמב"ם — *Rambam*, Rabbi Moses ben Maimon (1138–1204), a preeminent philosopher and physician</note>, of blessed memory, explained in the "Treatise on Health." And if he is accustomed to this, the Holy One, blessed be He, will protect him from all illness. And likewise regarding sleep: a man should not lie down to sleep while he is full until his food has rested a little in his bowels. And likewise washing and <term>bloodletting</term> <note>original: הקזה — *Hakaza*, a historical medical practice of withdrawing blood to prevent or cure illness</note>; he should not do those things one after the other because every change from the body's habit leads to danger, and may the Lord have pity and mercy upon us for His service, may His name be blessed. And also a man should guard himself from anger and from companions <gloss>and friends</gloss> who love "brothers sitting together" <note>An ironic reference to Psalm 133:1; here implying social gatherings that lead to distraction rather than holiness</note>, for it leads to foreign thoughts that nullify the service of the Name, may He be blessed. And every man must be ready to fulfill, according to his strength, <term>positive commandments</term> <note>original: מצות עשה — *Mitzvot Aseh*</note> that are time-bound and <term>prohibitive commandments</term> <note>original: מצות לא תעשה — *Mitzvot Lo Ta'aseh*</note>, so that he does not stumble, Heaven forbid, into forbidden things, especially the desecration of the Sabbath and Festivals. And likewise also regarding the matter of business and <term>commerce</term> <note>original: מו"מ — *Masah u-Matan*, literally "carrying and giving," the common term for business dealings</note>, that it should be in faith and with a correct weight, and let there be no deception, Heaven forbid, and the Lord will make his way prosper. And it is written that whoever does these explained things, "I promise him <gloss>with certainty</gloss> that he will dwell with a secure heart and will not fear." Security in the Name, may He be blessed, through the love of kindness; and the Holy One, blessed be He, will also rejoice in him who loves kindness, for "He who has mercy on [fellow] creatures, mercy is shown to him from Heaven." And a person should always remember all the above in his heart so that he does not sin before his Creator, who promises good things to Israel. And happy is he who comes to the World to Come with his learning in his hand, and eternal light shall dwell in his portion <unclear>and his grace</unclear>.

<meta>Continuity Note: The previous page concluded a series of ethical and mystical instructions regarding memory and conduct. This page transitions into a dense, scholarly analysis of Rabbinic sources, specifically resolving contradictions and variant readings in the works of early legal authorities like Rabbeinu Chananel and the authors of the Tur and Beit Yosef.</meta>

### 42
Regarding what was written in the <term>Magen Avraham</term> <note>original: מ"ח — refers to the *Magen Avraham*, a major commentary on the Shulchan Aruch</note>, section 3, concerning the variant reading in the <term>Benei Yonah</term> <note>refers to the students of Rabbi Yonah Gerondi or a specific edition of his work</note> that the mention of "Chananel" (of blessed memory) there is not the famous <term>Rabbeinu Chananel</term>. For it is written in the *Magen Avraham* that the term there is explained in the <term>common tongue</term> <note>original: בלע"ז — *be-la'az*, literally "in a foreign tongue," usually referring to the local vernacular</note>, namely in the German language, etc. And I find this very difficult based on what is written there, that this "Chananel" is not the famous one; for in the book *HaManhig* <note>The Leader, a 12th-century work by Rabbi Abraham ben Nathan</note> it is written in section 151: "The explanation of Chananel: he explained the matter of circumcision..." etc. See there. Thus, he explicitly mentioned Rabbeinu Chananel! 

However, one must recognize the lack of [manuscript] power regarding Rabbeinu Chananel in what is written here in the second volume of the <term>Tur</term> <note>The "Row," the foundational code of Jewish law by Rabbi Jacob ben Asher</note> in the section <term>Even HaEzer</term> <note>original: אה"ע — the section of Jewish law dealing with marriage and divorce</note>, section 83, where it says: "And so said Rabbeinu Chananel in what is written in the *Tur* section 1," etc. And the Great Sage <term>Maharash</term> <note>original: מהר"ש — likely Rabbi Shmuel Hadid or another contemporary scholar</note> (of blessed memory) wondered about this in section 16, in the glosses to section 63, where he wrote concerning what the *Tur* wrote there, and in the <term>Beit Yosef</term> <note>The "House of Joseph," the definitive commentary on the *Tur* by Joseph Karo</note> it is found there—see it there. If so, Rabbeinu Chananel is indeed mentioned there in the *Magen Avraham*, section 63, and these matters require clarification.

And regarding what the *Beit Yosef* wrote in this book, section 83: "And in the *Tur* there, Rabbeinu Chananel (of blessed memory) wrote that in the book *HaManhig* it says..." etc. However, the author of the <term>Benei Yonah</term> (of blessed memory) did not want to interpret it this way, and he wrote that what is mentioned as "Chananel" there is not Rabbeinu Chananel, but rather some other commentator named Chananel. And I have a great difficulty with his words, for Rabbeinu Chananel wrote explicitly in the book *HaManhig*, which concludes there with the words of "Rabbeinu Chananel, of blessed memory." Rather, it is certain that the intention of Rabbeinu Chananel is as written in the book *HaManhig*, for there the words of Rabbeinu Chananel are explicit. 

And it is as the *Tur* wrote in the aforementioned section, regarding what the *Beit Yosef* wrote there about the book *HaManhig*, noting that he heard from his teachers that the intention of Chananel is as written. And so wrote the Rabbi <term>Chida</term> <note>Rabbi Chaim Joseph David Azulai, a famous 18th-century polymath and bibliographer</note> (of blessed memory) in *Midbar Kedemot* <note>The Desert of Antiquity</note>, in the section for the letter *Het*, item 27, in the name of <term>Maharash Hadid</term> (of blessed memory) in the Responsa *Mayim Amukim* <note>Deep Waters</note>, section 86; see there. Also see in the Responsa *Divrei Mordechai*, section 64, who wrote that the reading of the *Benei Yonah* is correct; see there and examine it closely.

---

### 43
Regarding what was written in the *Magen Avraham*, section 10, that in the <term>Jerusalem Talmud</term> <note>The Palestinian Talmud, compiled earlier than the Babylonian one</note> at the beginning of the chapter *HaKones*, the Sages (of blessed memory) said that these *Okhvata* <note>marked with an x in manuscript</note> <note>A type of small fish, possibly anchovies or minnows</note> which are not fit for eating—and my teacher the Sage (of blessed memory) wrote there that this is called *Prayra* in the <term>common tongue</term> <note>original: בלע"ז — *be-la'az*</note>, while <term>Rabbeinu Yonah</term> (of blessed memory) wrote it as *Pragra* in the common tongue. 

And I have a great difficulty with him: for in our [Babylonian] Talmud, in the chapter *HaKones* on page 60b, it explains this explicitly as *Ahiya*. Why did my teacher the Sage and Rabbeinu Yonah (of blessed memory) not explain it according to the Talmud, which explains there that the <term>Amoraim</term> <note>The Sages of the Talmudic era</note> argue about it, one saying this and the other saying that? See there, as it requires further study. Perhaps we must say that what the Sages wrote is specifically what was known in our own language, and therefore they did not want to write the words of the Talmud. This requires further thought.

---

### 45
Regarding what was written in the *Magen Avraham*, section 10, that in the *Tur* section 123 it is written in the name of **Rabbi Isaiah** that a round piece of dung is not considered significant, etc. And because of this, the <term>Mordechai</term> <note>A major legal compendium by Rabbi Mordechai ben Hillel</note> wrote in the chapter *HaMotzi Yayin* in section 447 that these rounds are called... [as written] by the Sage <term>Maharash</term> (of blessed memory) in section 15. 

And I have a great difficulty with this, for in the *Beit Yosef* there in section 123, he cited this in the name of **Rabbi Isaiah di Trani** (of blessed memory), and he did not cite the words of the *Mordechai* at all. We must say that what is written in the book *HaManhig* in the name of Rabbi Isaiah is a different version <note>original: גירסא — *Girsa*, a textual variant</note> that the Sage (of blessed memory) saw, and the *Tur* brought his words in the aforementioned section. And what is written in the book *Knesset HaGedola* <note>The Great Assembly, a commentary by Rabbi Chaim Benveniste</note> on section 123 in the glosses to the *Tur*, item 8, follows the words of the aforementioned *Mordechai*. See there and examine it closely.

The author focuses on resolving discrepancies between the Beit Yosef, the Tur, and other authorities like Rabbeinu Chananel and Rabbi Isaiah di Trani.</summary>

<meta>Continuity Note: There is a significant thematic shift here. The previous page (37) focused on complex legal (Halakhic) debates regarding Rabbeinu Chananel and the Tur. <margin>36</margin>
Every place where this attainment exists—namely, the love of God and His Torah—is in the aspect of <term>Adam</term> <note>Man</note>. For <term>Adam</term> is named after the phrase, "I will be like the Most High" <note>original: אדמה לעליון — *Adameh le-Elyon*; a play on words from Isaiah 14:14 where *Adam* (man) is linked to the verb *adameh* (I will resemble/be like)</note>. And this is the aspect of <term>Adam</term>, for man is the one who raises and lowers the life-force <note>original: חיות — *chayut*, the vital divine energy</note> to all existing things. And this is in the aspect of the "Pillar of Thought," through which every aspect of subtle thought and the aspect of speech—in that man is defined by speech—is clarified. This is the true aspect of <term>Adam</term>. For the intellect of man is an aspect of Divinity. 

A person must strive to perceive the intellect of the Blessed Creator in everything. This is "perfect intellect." Through this, man is cleaved to the true Life. This is the meaning of "A good understanding to all who perform them" <note>Psalm 111:10</note>. Through this, the <term>Shekhinah</term> <note>the Divine Presence</note> dwells within the person, in the aspect of "Make for Me a sanctuary, and I will dwell among them" <note>Exodus 25:8</note>—meaning, within each and every individual. This is the primary purpose of man's creation: that he should gain this insight. This is the aspect of intellect and thought—to be constantly cleaved to his Creator, may His name be blessed. Through this, the <term>Shekhinah</term>, as it were, will be included with all the souls in their root, and this is a complete union.

---

<margin>110</margin>
However, behold, as is known regarding this Holy and Awesome Name; for the Holy and Awesome Name dwells [within], and is also the absolute Unity. It is the simple Intellect in this place, from which the vitality of the <term>Shekhinah</term> is formed and takes hold, in the aspect of the honored and awesome Name <term>YHVH</term> <note>the four-letter Name of God, often referred to as the Tetragrammaton</note>. See the Midrash Rabbah. In truth, the aspect of the Name is what dwells within the Children of Israel, as it is written: "The Lord is among them, Sinai is in holiness" <note>Psalm 68:18</note>. This shows that the Blessed Creator dwells within the Children of Israel. Therefore, every man must see to it that he provides a "dwelling place" for the Name, and that the Name should be a sanctuary for a dwelling, where the person shall be hidden—that his union, his constant thought, his speech, and his actions should all be for the glory of the Blessed Creator, may His great Name be blessed. By this, he causes a perfect connection, so that the Name becomes a sanctuary for the dwelling of the pure soul. 

This is the aspect of the World of <term>Binah</term> <note>Understanding; the third of the ten Sephirot or divine emanations</note>, where "thought" exists in the aspect of "Good news fattens the bone" <note>Proverbs 15:30; original: שמועה טובה תדשן גרם — *shmu'ah tovah tedashen garem*</note>. "News" (*shmu'ah*) relates to hearing and understanding (*Binah*), as it were—the proper thought of a man who contemplates his words and feels the "hearing" within the thought. Through this, he gives power and strength to the supernal, pure soul, which is a sanctuary for the <term>Shekhinah</term>. He awakens the aspect of supernal "hearing"—"You shall surely listen to My voice" <note>Exodus 19:5</note>. He also awakens [this] above, so that the <term>Shekhinah</term> will be, as it were, in the aspect of "hearing" to receive the speech in the aspect of "good news"—and this is enough for the wise. This is the meaning of "Good news fattens the bone," for through his speech and pure thought, he draws an illumination from the <term>Shekhinah</term> to the aspect of his own essence and "bones" <note>his physical frame</note>. This is also the aspect of "Happy are those who dwell in Your house" <note>Psalm 84:5</note>—that his dwelling place should always be cleaved to the Blessed Creator, out of awe and love. Through this, he is included within the <term>Shekhinah</term>. This is the secret of the verse, "Blessed be the Lord from Zion" <note>Psalm 135:21</note>, for here the <term>Shekhinah</term> dwells in the aspect of Zion. "And from Zion, the Torah shall go forth." This is enough for the wise; understand it.

---

<margin>130</margin>
Behold, when a man contemplates with his intellect and the aspect of knowledge <note>original: דעת — *Da'at*</note> that he receives vitality from the Blessed Creator at every single moment, and that He literally dwells within his body—through this, he will reach great humility and the "nullification of the self" <note>original: ביטול היש — *bittul ha-yesh*, the mystical ego-death or self-abnegation</note>. For he nullifies his entire body to the vitality of the Blessed Creator. Through this, he is included in the <term>Or Ein Sof</term> <note>the Infinite Light of God</note>. And this is enough for the wise.

<summary>This page explores the mystical connection between man and God, arguing that the human intellect is a piece of Divinity and that by purifying thought and speech, a person becomes a sanctuary for the Divine Presence (Shekhinah). It concludes that recognizing God as the constant source of one's vitality leads to total humility and union with the Infinite Light.</summary>

<meta>Continuity Note: The previous page concluded a discussion on the definition of "Man" (Adam) as a being capable of reflecting the Divine through intellect and speech. This page transitions from the nature of Man to the nature of Divine sustenance, specifically the mystical significance of "Bread" and "The Table," using the Kabbalistic teachings of Rabbi Joseph Gikatilla to explain how physical nourishment connects to the spiritual flow of Lovingkindness.</meta>

"The kindness of God is all the day" <note>original: חסד אל כל היום — *Chesed El kol hayom*; Psalm 52:3</note>, and as is written in this gate in the sixth discourse: prior to any creation, there is the **Set Table** <note>original: השלחן הערוך — *HaShulchan HaArukh*; referring here to Divine provision prepared for all beings</note>, ready for all their needs, etc. From here it is explained how and when the tithe was separated from the "dough" of the **Kingdom** <note>original: מלכות — *Malchut*; the lowest of the ten Divine emanations, associated with the physical world and the feminine principle</note>, for these first two arrangements are attained. Its eighth part, and the "Table" aspect within it, is this gate, which is above this level called "plain bread" <note>original: פת סתם — *pat stam*</note>. Rather, it belongs to the **Bread of the Presence** <note>original: לחם הפנים — *Lechem HaPanim*; the twelve loaves displayed in the ancient Temple</note>, etc. Behold, the Table is arranged within His Palace, including the six extremities <note>the six spatial directions</note> called the "six breads," and from here we shall explain the language of the Mishnah: **Whole Bread** <note>original: פת שלימה — *pat shleimah*</note>.

And so we find in the commentary of Rabbi Joseph Gikatilla, of blessed memory <note>A prominent 13th-century Spanish Kabbalist</note>, on the Midrashic statement in the chapter "Katan": "A dry morsel," etc. For behold, that loaf is "whole" in its bread, for it draws from the bread of the Holy One, Blessed be He, which has the numerical value of **eighteen** <note>original: ח"י — *Chai*, meaning "Life." In the Hebrew system of Gematria, letters have numerical values: Chet=8, Yud=10, totaling 18</note>. And its portion is eighteen, which we call the **Bread of the Presence**, which is "whole" because the side of **Lovingkindness** <note>original: חסד — *Chesed*</note> is added to it. This is within the secret of the "forty-nine faces of purity" and "forty-nine faces of impurity," where within every "face" of **Judgment** <note>original: דין — *Din*; the attribute of severity or restriction</note> exists this attribute of the **Kingdom** relative to this level called "plain bread." There, the bread is "whole" in the same way we recite a blessing over "whole bread," and as I will write later regarding the secret of the distribution of portions on the Sabbath. This is the meaning of the phrase **Whole Bread**.

---

<margin>
18
</margin>
->18<-

Referencing the verse **"And God saw the light, that it was good"** <note>Genesis 1:4</note>. The explanation is that this level is "Eighteen" <note>Chai/Life</note>. Here, the word **"Good"** <note>original: טו"ב — *Tov*</note> is explained: it is from the word that awakens and illuminates **Peace** <note>original: שלום — *Shalom*</note> in general. "Eighteen" itself is a level called **Peace**, and it is the level that binds and unites everything. It is known that in every "peace" there is a hidden thing. The same applies to the attribute called **Good**, in the secret of that which is "hidden for the righteous in the future to come," as mentioned in the Zohar. And here it hints to **"And God said, 'Let there be light,' and there was light"** <note>Genesis 1:3</note>, and he explained the matter of "Good," for that original light was hidden away for the righteous in the world to come.

---

And now we shall explain the word **"Morsel"** <note>original: פת — *Pat*; also translated as "bread"</note> on its own. Know that every aspect called **Morsel** is called a "level," and he clarified the nature of its vowel-pointing as the level of **Opening** <note>original: פתח — *Petach*; a play on the word *Pat* (bread) and the Hebrew vowel *Patach*, which means 'opening'</note>. For every "opening" has an aspect of beginning: *"Open for me the gates of righteousness"* <note>Psalm 118:19</note>. As is known, through this the treasury of Good and Lovingkindness is opened, flowing from Him downward. This is the name **Morsel** in its Supernal Root, in the aspect of what they, of blessed memory, wrote regarding **The Opening of Elijah** <note>original: פתח אליהו — *Petach Eliyahu*; a famous passage from the Zohar often recited before prayer</note>. For through this "Opening," the power of flow and the will to bestow and emanate to the created beings is opened.

Behold, the **Morsel** indicates the "Opening" and the "Flow." There is, above, total Lovingkindness and also the attribute of harsh Judgment. The aspect of this **Morsel** is the gate to open the source of Lovingkindness and to sweeten the felt Judgment. Because this level includes both Judgment and Lovingkindness, it is called **Morsel** at the level of Lovingkindness and Judgment. It is called the **Whole Morsel** because there the bread of Lovingkindness is complete. It is "whole/complete" in the sense of the verse *"And God saw the light..."* And this is the meaning of the phrase **Whole Bread**. In the secret of the Judgment within it, it is an "Opening" <note>Petach</note> to sweeten the judgments and draw Lovingkindness into them. For anyone who gives **Charity** <note>original: צדקה — *Tzedakah*</note> opens an "Opening" of Lovingkindness to sweeten the absolute Judgment from Above. All this is in the aspect of this attribute called **Whole Bread**.
