It uses the metaphor of a person standing by a restless sea to describe the perpetual vigilance required to avoid sin.</summary>

Whoever takes refuge under the wings of the Divine Presence <term>Shekhinah: The indwelling or settled presence of God</term>—for every pious and holy person, this royal garment is an honor when departing from this world on the day of his death, as he passes from us.

He shall enter in peace, and they shall rest upon their couches—those righteous ones who walk uprightly. And may it be the will of the God Most High, who dwells above—the God of truth—that kindness and truth shall guide him and this holy congregation with complete kindness and great mercy, Amen. For the hand of the Lord was upon my heart to inform you that the Lord God of Israel is ~~the truth of righteousness~~ ~~such as sin~~. Stand and contemplate the Holy Torah given to Moses His servant by the angel of the Lord of Hosts, and from within this great fire which they heard in actual prophecy on the day of the Giving of the Torah <term>Matan Torah: The revelation of the Law at Mount Sinai</term>, a very faithful testimony—the oath in this world and the next—to learn and to teach. For behold, on this day the Master of the Universe, blessed be He, was revealed to us, and everything said to me is clear, lucid, and true, as it is written in the Torah of Moses, the servant of the Lord:

> In the third month after the children of Israel had gone out of the land of Egypt, on this day they came to the wilderness of Sinai. For they departed from Rephidim, and had come to the wilderness of Sinai, and had camped in the wilderness; and there Israel camped before the mountain. And Moses went up to God, and the Lord called to him from the mountain, saying, "Thus you shall say to the house of Jacob, and tell the children of Israel: You have seen what I did to the Egyptians, and how I bore you on eagles' wings and brought you to Myself. Now therefore, if you will indeed obey My voice and keep My covenant, then you shall be a special treasure to Me above all people; for all the earth is Mine. And you shall be to Me a kingdom of priests and a holy nation." These are the words which you shall speak to the children of Israel. <note>original: Exodus 19:1-6</note>

Behold, I say to you that the great name of the Holy One, blessed be He, dwells among us.

<meta>This page continues the spiritual guidance from the previous section. While the previous page focused on the "Shiviti" (constant awareness of God) and the rewards for the righteous in the afterlife, this text transitions into a specific pedagogical treatise attributed to Saint Ephrem the Syrian, focusing on the mechanics and internal disposition required for true prayer.</meta>

# The Teaching of Saint Mar Ephrem

We begin, with the help of God, the teaching of the holy Mar Ephrem <term>Mar: A Syriac title of respect meaning 'Lord' or 'Saint'</term>, the Syrian Teacher, regarding what is learned from him concerning prayer: how we should pray, and how the soul of a person behaves in each instance—whether by the sound of the voice that speaks, or in the thought <term>Maḥšabta: The internal meditation or 'thought' of the heart</term> that exists within.

### An Explanation of Ephrem’s Words on Prayer

From all his teachings on prayer, one must learn how it may be filled with true intent. Whether in the heart, which is constant in prayer, or in the place

->**Of Prayer**<-

wherever one may be during the time of prayer. He says that children should descend <note>likely referring to students or 'children of the faith'</note> just as he said he has something to teach you regarding how you pray. 

You should know whether it is by voice, as a man who has something to learn by the sound of his words, or in thought, so that he might be as one who truly prays. At every time of prayer, he has something to learn; sometimes he acts as the teacher, and sometimes as the knower. 

At every time of prayer, as he said, there is something to learn. How is it when he prays? Is it a prayer of thought, or is it by voice like a man who speaks? And how shall he stand in it? He says that sometimes when a man stands to pray, **his thought wanders to another place; his body stands in prayer, but his heart is far from it.** 

As it is said, he possesses only the outward knowledge. Even if he says he is praying, he is praying without knowledge <term>Ida’ta: Spiritual insight or conscious understanding</term>. Sometimes, while standing to pray, the thought runs to another place, and while his body remains standing in prayer, his heart is at a great distance from it. How then shall he stand in prayer?

---

<margin>Ephrem</margin>

An explanation of his words on how prayer is constituted and how one should pray: If a man stands to pray and his heart is far from the prayer, his prayer is not accepted. As he said, if a man prays without the knowledge of God, his prayer is not accepted. 

He said that "the children should descend," meaning the person should be present in the place of prayer. He explains the nature of prayer: sometimes when one stands to pray, he speaks from the voice of his heart; at such a time, his prayer is accepted. 

But if the prayer is only in the thought while it wanders to another place, and his body stands in prayer while his heart is far from it, his prayer is not accepted. He said that we should pray at all times—whether by voice, like a man speaking, or in thought, so that he may be as one truly praying. At every time of prayer, he has something to learn: how to pray from the soul, whether by the sound of the voice or in the internal thought. Otherwise, his body stands in prayer while his heart is far from it, and his prayer is not accepted. After these things, he said:

<summary>This page introduces a treatise by Saint Ephrem the Syrian on the dual nature of prayer (vocal vs. mental) and warns against "absent-minded" prayer where the body is present but the heart is distant.</summary>
<keywords>Mar Ephrem, prayer, Syriac Christianity, intention, heart vs body, spiritual knowledge, meditation, liturgical practice</keywords>

<meta>This page transitions from the Syriac teachings of Ephrem the Syrian into a Jewish homiletic and mystical commentary. May the hand of the Lord be upon His work which He has performed, and in the miracles He granted here in the cycle of what He sent into his heart; and let it be with miracles and wonders, and also upon them, and at the end:
> And over all that was done in the work of the Tabernacle <term>Mishkan: The portable earthly dwelling place of God’s presence during the Exodus</term>, etc.
It appears here in the soul that He shall place wonders within his heart.

---

And thus for me, blessed be His name, it shall not be for a shelter or a fortified city to act <note>original: "למחסה ולעיר מבצר לעשות"</note>,
And behold, the Divine Presence <term>Shekhinah: The feminine aspect of the Divine that dwells among humanity</term> shall dwell in the house.
And our Sages of Blessed Memory have already written: as long as the soul is within a person, he should cling to the commandments of his Creator.
And our Sages of Blessed Memory have already written: whomever the Almighty loves, He pours influence upon him and causes him to see Him through the commandments.
> For the Torah is a "Tree of Life" <note>original: "עץ חיים", referencing Proverbs 3:18</note> to those who hold fast to it, and one should speak of it day and night, and in its end is life.
> "A river flows out of Eden..." etc. This is the light of the Divine Presence, and the body of the commandment is drawn from it.
> And likewise, "For the commandment is a lamp and the Torah is light" <note>original: "נר מצוה ותורה אור", quoting Proverbs 6:23</note>, etc.
For what also comes from this is the aspect of the commandments.
And also that the lamp is the Torah, and the wick is the soul.
And they shall be blessed for them,
And may there be peace and tranquility.

---

> May it be the will of the Almighty that a man’s days and years should be in pleasantness and peace.
But when they commit a transgression, God forbid, behold, in the soul is the measure <note>original: "בנפש המדה", perhaps referring to the internal balance of character</note>, for it shall not rest.
And the soul is called "half of the Name" <term>The "Name" refers to God (HaShem). This mystical idea suggests the soul is a literal spark or portion of the Divine</term> of the Blessed One, and thus it has no rest during [the neglect of] service.
This is a hint to those things which are in our hands.

---

And the Rif <term>Rabbi Isaac Alfasi, a dominant 11th-century North African legal scholar</term>, of blessed memory, has already written: the matter of the Divine Presence in the house is of the highest good.
And we have already explained above that the matter of Divine service is in the place of prayer and in one's home.
And one makes a remembrance and an order: "I shall prepare for you in your place," etc.
And we have already seen regarding this what the Zohar <term>The primary text of Jewish mysticism (Kabbalah)</term> wrote in the portion of *Vayechi* on the verse (Genesis 49):
> "Simeon and Levi are brothers," etc.
This means to say: the matter of the commandment and the body of the prayer are the very same matter.
As mentioned above in the aspect of the man who sinned and slaughtered his blemish, and blemished the altar and the sacrifice—and who caused this?
Isaac, the master of the soul and the attributes, which are Kindness, Strength, and Beauty <term>Hesed, Gevurah, and Tiferet: The three primary emotional "Sefirot" or divine emanations in Kabbalah</term>, for there...
And we have already explained the matter of the soul and the body of man, for the Blessed Name placed it in the heart of man.
Taste it for yourselves and he shall say; he shall not say anything except "grace and kindness in the eyes of God and man."
> And King David, peace be upon him, said: "The heavens are the heavens of the Lord... and the earth He gave to the children of men" <note>original: "השמים שמים להשם... והארץ נתן לבני אדם", quoting Psalm 115:16</note>.
And therefore, through the action that a man performs here below, he causes light [to shine] above; and the opposite, God forbid.
> May it be His will that we merit to see the face of our righteous Messiah speedily in our days, Amen.
And the explanation of what is written: "And Enoch walked with God," etc. <note>original: "ויתהלך חנוך את האלהים", quoting Genesis 5:24</note>.
